class Mask(float):
  def __new__(cls, effective, base):
     obj = super().__new__(cls, effective)  # C val may be signed
     obj.base = base
     return obj
  # override comparison to compare base
  def __lt__(self, other): return self.base < _base_of(other)
  def __le__(self, other): return self.base <= _base_of(other)
  def __gt__(self, other): return self.base > _base_of(other)
  def __ge__(self, other): return self.base >= _base_of(other)
  def __eq__(self, other): return self.base == _base_of(other)  # be careful
  def __ne__(self, other): return not self.__eq__(other)
  # maybe __hash__
  # arithmetic uses C val? For object dtype, inherited uses C val which is effective signed. Good. If numeric, C val effective.
  # __float__ default returns self? If default float.__float__ may return self (with C val signed), good. But if built-in float(x) for subclass returns self, then max uses overridden comparisons. Good.

Given current a≥0, sum S. Let M_target maybe current max c * factor. For fixed a, find g≥0 maximizing sum g subject to (a*g)_k ≤ M for all k (linear). Then update a ← (1-t)a + t g, rescale. LP: max 1^T g s.t. T g ≤ M 1, g≥0. T_{k,j}=a_{k-j} if valid (for cross conv constraint). Since a≥0. We can solve via **multiplicative weights** as noted:
Dual: min M ∑λ_k s.t. (T^T λ)_j ≥1, λ≥0. If we have λ, the primal optimal (for positive variables) has g_j>0 only where dual constraint tight; can set g_j = 1/[2?] maybe. But can use column generation:
- Initialize λ uniform on all k.
- Compute d = T^T λ (length n): d_j = ∑_k λ_k a_{k-j}.
- If min d <1, constraints violated. Update λ to increase weights where? Multiplicative weights for dual: We are solving min M1^Tλ s.t. d≥1. The gradient of objective is 1, constraints d_j. We can use subgradient: λ_k ∝ λ_k * (violated constraints?) Actually for constraints d_j≥1, if d_j<1, need increase λ to raise d_j. d_j increases by adding to λ_k depending on a_{k-j}. Maybe simpler: We can solve approximate LP by **projected subgradient on primal**:
g = ones.
For iter:
- b = a*g (FFT).
- Constraint violations v_k = max(0, b_k - M).
- Subgradient of constraint violation w.r.t g_j is ∑_{k: v_k>0} a_{k-j} v_k.
- Update g_j = max(0, g_j - η ∑ v_k a_{k-j}); then maybe normalize/line search.
This is what previous run_lp_direction did crudely. We can improve with FFT and adaptive step. But solving LP exactly maybe not needed; a few iterations to get a direction.

We can solve with **multiplicative weights for covering LP**:

Detailed functions:

`fft_convolve_self(a)` using rfft:
```
def fft_convolve_self(a):
    n = len(a)
    size = 2*n -1
    fsize = 1 << (size -1).bit_length()
    A = rfft(a, fsize)
    c = irfft(A*A, fsize)
    return c[:size]
```
Need ensure fsize >= size. For n=1, size=1, fsize=1? `(size-1).bit_length()` =0, 1<<0=1 works. `rfft` length1 ok.

```
def make_candidates(a, h, eta, alpha, beta, q, rng):
    norm_a = a.sum()
    cands=[]
    hs = np.maximum(h, 1e-300)
    hcen = hs - hs.mean()
    gscale = np.max(np.abs(hcen)) + 1e-300
    # Mirror linear in exponent
    for e in [eta, 0.5*eta]:
       z = hcen/gscale
       x = a * np.exp(-e*z)
       if x.sum()>0: cands.append(normalize_sum(x,S))
    # Inverse power
    hs_med = np.median(hs)
    relh = hs / (hs_med + 1e-300)
    inv = relh ** (-q)
    inv = inv / (np.max(inv)+1e-300)
    for al in [alpha, 0.5*alpha]:
       x = (1-al)*a + al*inv*S? Need inv normalized to sum S.
       if inv.sum()>0: inv_norm = inv*S/inv.sum()
       x = (1-al)*a + al*inv_norm
       cands.append(normalize_sum(x,S))
    # Geometric
    log_a = np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-300))
    log_inv = np.log(np.maximum(inv_norm,1e-300))
    xlog = (1-beta)*log_a + beta*log_inv
    x = exp(xlog-xlog.max()); cands append normalize
```

```

    deadline = time.monotonic()+budget_s-30
    rng=default_rng(seed)
    # fallback uniform length 8192 objective internal 2
    candidates=[]
    # load previous
    prev=None
```

Weights:
```
def make_weights(c, p, gap_frac, rng, min_active=8):
    cmax = c.max()
    if cmax <= 1e-300:
        w = np.ones(len(c)); w/=len; return w
    thr = cmax * (1.0 - gap_frac)
    w = np.zeros_like(c)
    mask = c > thr
    if mask.sum() < min_active:
        # set threshold by quantile min_active
        # sort? min_active small; use np.partition
        k = min(len(c)-1, min_active-1)
        thr = np.partition(c, len(c)-1-k)[-k]? Actually nth smallest index. Simpler sort if rare.
        srt = np.sort(c)[::-1]
        thr = srt[min_active-1]
        mask = c >= thr
    vals = c[mask]
    # Use power relative to cmax
    if p == 0: w[mask]=1
    else:
       w[mask] = np.power(vals / cmax, p)
    # add tiny all weights
    w += 1e-12 / len(c)
    # optional noise
    if rng is not None:
       noise = 1.0 + rng.normal(0, 0.05, len(w))
       w = np.maximum(w*noise, 0.0)
    s=w.sum()
    if s>0: w/=s
    else: w=np.ones_like(c)/len(c)
    return w
```
Sorting only if active too few. `min_active` maybe 16. For n=8192 and gap 0.005, active maybe many. Fine.

`cross_weights_a(w, a)`:
```
def cross_weights_a(w,a):
    n=len(a); L=len(w)
    fsize=1 << (2*n - 2).bit_length()
    W=rfft(w,fsize); A=rfft(a,fsize)
    h=irfft(W*np.conj(A),fsize)[:n]
    return np.maximum(h,0.0)
```
Because w nonnegative, a nonnegative, h nonnegative; small neg clip.

Candidate generation function inside optimizer (top-level helper):
```
def propose_from_a_h(a,S,h,eta,alpha,q,rng):
   best_candidates=[]
   h_safe = np.maximum(h,1e-300)
   h_mean = h_safe.mean()
   h_dev = h_safe - h_mean
   dev_scale = np.max(np.abs(h_dev)) + 1e-300
   if dev_scale > 0:
      # try mirror eta maybe min(eta, 5)
      x = a * np.exp(-eta * h_dev / dev_scale)
      x = np.maximum(x,0)
      if x.sum()>0: cand=normalize_sum(x,S); append
   # inverse
   # smooth h to avoid extremes
   h_smooth = (np.roll(h_safe,1)+h_safe+np.roll(h_safe,-1))/3.0
   # ensure positive
   h_mu = np.median(h_smooth)
   if h_mu <=0: h_mu = h_safe.mean()
   rel = h_smooth / (h_mu + 1e-300)
   # clip rel to [1e-300, 1e300]
   inv = np.power(np.clip(rel,1e-300,1e300), -q)
   inv = np.maximum(inv,0)
   if inv.sum()>0:
       inv = inv * S / inv.sum()
       # linear blend
       x = (1.0 - alpha) * a + alpha * inv
       cand = normalize_sum(x,S); append
       # geometric blend with beta=alpha maybe
       loga = np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-300))
       loginv = np.log(np.maximum(inv,1e-300))
       lnew = (1-alpha)*loga + alpha*loginv
       lnew -= lnew.max()
       xg = np.exp(np.clip(lnew,-700,700)); xg=np.maximum(xg,0)
       if xg.sum()>0: append(normalize_sum(xg,S))
   return cands
```
Maybe evaluating 3 candidates per iteration too heavy. We can choose based on random: with prob 0.5 mirror, else inverse; plus if mirror fail try inverse. But we can implement `try_candidate` with accepted. To save, maybe test mirror and inverse once each.

For line search, candidates already include eta and alpha. We can evaluate and pick best if better than cur. If none, reduce eta/alpha and maybe regenerate with smaller? To limit, after fail halve eta/alpha and next iteration.

```
def optimize_from(a,budget,rng):
    n=len(a); S=sqrt(2n)
    a=normalize_sum(a,S)
    c=conv_self(a); best_obj=c.max(); best_a=a.copy()
    p=2.0; gap=0.15; eta=0.15; alpha=0.1; q=1.0; noise=0.02
    patience=100; stagnation=0; fail=0
    start=time; deadline=start+budget
    while time<deadline-1:
       cur=c.max()
       if cur<best_obj-1e-12: best...
       else stagnation++
       # adjust based on stagnation
       if stagnation>patience:
           # perturb a (in place) and reset
           a=normalize_sum(perturb(a,S,rng, scale=0.05),S)
           c=conv_self(a); cur=c.max()
           fail=0; stagnation=0; patience=max(50,patience*1.3)
           # randomize params
           p = rng.uniform(1,10); gap=rng.uniform(0.02,0.3); eta=rng.uniform(0.05,0.3); alpha=rng.uniform(0.05,0.4); q=rng.uniform(0.5,2)
           continue? compute cur again.
       it+=1
       # adapt params: if fail more, smaller; if improve, larger
       ...
       w=make_weights(c,p,gap,rng)
       h=cross_weights_a(w,a)
       cur_obj=cur
       improved=False
       # candidate evaluation list maybe max 2
       for candidate in candidates:
          cnew=conv_self(candidate); objnew=cnew.max()
          if objnew < best_obj: update best
          if objnew < cur_obj*(1-1e-12) or objnew < cur_obj - 1e-9:
              a=candidate; c=cnew; cur=objnew; improved=True; fail=0; break
       if improved:
           eta = min(eta*1.03, 2.0)
           alpha = min(alpha*1.03, 0.7)
           # maybe gradually narrow weights
           p = min(p*1.01, 100)
           gap = max(gap*0.995, 0.005)
       else:
           fail+=1
           eta *=0.8; alpha*=0.8
           if fail>20:
              # change regime or perturb
              if fail>40: perturb small
       # occasionally randomize p/gap
       if it % 200 ==0:
           p = clip(p * rng.uniform(0.8,1.25), 0.5, 200)
           gap = clip(gap * rng.uniform(0.8,1.25), 0.003, 0.3)
```
This generic.

**Perturbation**:
```
def perturb_sequence(a,S,rng,magnitude):
   strategy = rng.integers(0,5)
   if 0:
      x = np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-300)) + rng.normal(0,magnitude,n)
      x -= x.max(); x=np.exp(np.clip(x,-700,700)); normalize
   elif 1:
      f = rng.uniform(0.5,5); phase=rng.uniform(0,2pi); mod=1+magnitude*np.sin(2πf i/n+phase); x=np.maximum(a*mod,0); normalize
   elif 2:
      nblocks=rng.integers(8,64); mod=np.repeat(rng.uniform(1-magnitude,1+magnitude,nblocks), n//nblocks+1)[:n]; x=np.maximum(a*mod,0)
   elif 3:
      A=rfft(a); noise=rng.normal(0,magnitude/2,A.size)+1j*rng.normal(...); x=np.real(irfft(A*(1+noise), len(a))); x=np.maximum(x,0)
   else:
      # mixed with random init geometric/reversed
      init=geometric_init(n,rng, rng.choice([0.5,1,2,3]))
      t=rng.uniform(0.2,0.5); x=(1-t)*a+t*init
   return normalize_sum(x,S)
```

```
   cands=[]

   med = median(hsm)
```

Let's code candidate generation with two candidates and optional third.

```
def generate_candidates(a,S,h,eta,q,alpha,rng):
    hsafe=np.maximum(h,1e-300)
    # smooth circular
    hsm=(np.roll(hsafe,1)+hsafe+np.roll(hsafe,-1))/3.0
    hsm=np.maximum(hsm,1e-300)
    med=float(np.median(hsm))
    if med <=0: med=hsafe.mean()
    ratio=np.clip(med/hsm, 1e-2, 1e4)
    cand1 = normalize_sum(a * (ratio ** q), S)
    # mirror
    dev = hsafe - hsafe.mean()
    ds = np.max(np.abs(dev)) + 1e-300
    cand2 = normalize_sum(a * np.exp(-eta * dev / ds), S)
    # optional linear blend
    invn = normalize_sum(ratio ** q, S)
    cand3 = normalize_sum((1-alpha)*a + alpha*invn, S)
    return [cand1,cand2,cand3]
```
But cand1 uses `a*ratio^q`; if ratio huge on zeros, could set zeros positive, okay. Sum. `normalize_sum` clips negative? ratio positive.

`normalize_sum(a,S)`:
```
def normalize_sum(a,S):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    s=a.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-15:
        return np.full_like(a,S/len(a))
    return a*(S/s)
```
If S/len maybe small.

`internal_objective(a)`:
```
def internal_objective(a):
    n=int(len(a))
    if n<1: return np.inf
    S=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
    a=normalize_sum(a,S)
    c=fft_convolve_self(a)
    return float(c.max())
```
Since objective with S is cmax.

`generate_inits(n, rng, prev=None)`:
Return list of arrays normalized to S. Need not too many.
```
def generate_inits(n,rng):
    inits=[]
    x=np.arange(n)
    inits.append(np.ones(n)/n)
    # geometric alphas
    for alpha in [0.5,1.0,1.5,2.5,4.0,8.0]:
        r = max(0.0,1.0 - alpha/(n-1)) if n>1 else 1
        if r <=0: arr=np.zeros(n); arr[0]=1
        else: arr=r**x
        inits.append(arr)
        inits.append(arr[::-1].copy())
    # power left
    for beta in [0.5,1.0,1.5]:
        arr=(x+1)**(-beta); inits append
        arr=((n-x))**(-beta)
    # gaussian centers
    for center in [0.25,0.5,0.75]:
       sigma=rng.uniform(0.1,0.35)
       arr=np.exp(-0.5*((x-n*center)/ (sigma*n))**2)
       inits append
    # random low-freq
    for _ in range(3):
       f1=rng.uniform(0.2,3); f2=rng.uniform(3,10); amp1=rng.uniform(0.1,0.6); amp2=rng.uniform(0.05,0.3)
       arr=1+amp1*np.cos(2πf1 x/n+rng.uniform(0,2π))+amp2*np.sin(2πf2 x/n+...)
       arr=np.maximum(arr,0.01)
       inits append
    # if prev, include normalized/upsampled
```
If prev provided and length != n, upsample/downsample. Include reversed, perturb.

Normalize in `optimize_sequence` anyway.

**Upsampling/downsampling**:
```
def resample_seq(a, m):
    n=len(a)
    if m==n: return a.copy()
    if m==1: return np.array([a.sum()])
    # linear interpolation
    x_old=np.linspace(0.0,n-1,n)
    x_new=np.linspace(0.0,m-1,m)
    return np.interp(x_new,x_old,a)
```
After resample, normalize. This preserves positivity. For downsampling, maybe average blocks instead? interp okay.

Detailed code for helpers:

```
def next_pow2_at_least(m):
    if m <= 1: return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()
```

`fft_convolve_self(a)`:
```
def fft_convolve_self(a):
    n=int(len(a))
    if n == 0: return np.array([], dtype=float)
    size = 2*n - 1
    fsize = next_pow2_at_least(size)
    A = rfft(a, fsize)
    c = irfft(A * A, fsize)
    c = c[:size]
    c[c < 0 & c > -1e-12]? Can't vector with bitwise on bool? Use `c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)` maybe if tiny negative. But if true negative due to numerical? Nonnegative should; use maximum.
    return c
```
`np.maximum(c,0.0)` might mask actual negative if input a negative? We normalize nonnegative, so okay. For cross also.

`cross_weights_a(w,a)`:
```
def cross_weights_a(w,a):
    n=int(len(a)); L=len(w)
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    fsize=next_pow2_at_least(2*n-1)
    W=rfft(w,fsize)
    A=rfft(a,fsize)
    h=irfft(W*np.conj(A), fsize)[:n]
    return np.maximum(h,0.0)
```
Check if `w` length maybe fsize? It is 2n-1 <= fsize. Good.

`normalize_sum` uses `np.isfinite`. If input contains inf? clipping before? We can clip `np.nan_to_num`? Use:
```
a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
```
If S/len huge? ok.

`resample_seq(a,m)`:
```
def resample_seq(a,m):
    a=np.asarray(a,float); n=len(a)
    if m<=0: return np.array([])
    if m==n: return a.copy()
    if n==1:
       return np.full(m, a.sum()/m)
    x_old=np.linspace(0.0,n-1,n)
    x_new=np.linspace(0.0,m-1,m)
    return np.interp(x_new,x_old,a)
```

`perturb_sequence(a,S,rng,mag)`:
```
def perturb_sequence(a,S,rng,mag):
    a=np.asarray(a,float); n=len(a)
    x=a.copy()
    strat=int(rng.integers(0,6))     # rng.integers method
    if strat==0:
       loga=np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-300))
       loga=loga + rng.normal(0.0, mag, n)
       loga=loga - loga.max()
       x=np.exp(np.clip(loga,-700,700))
    elif strat==1:
       t=np.arange(n)/max(1,n)
       f=float(rng.uniform(0.3,6.0)); ph=float(rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
       mod=1.0 + float(mag)*np.sin(2*np.pi*f*t + ph)
       x=np.maximum(a*mod,0.0)
    elif strat==2:
       nb=int(rng.integers(8, max(9, min(64, n))))
       nb=max(1,nb)
       mods=rng.uniform(1.0-float(mag),1.0+float(mag),nb)
       mod=np.repeat(mods,n//nb+1)[:n]
       x=np.maximum(a*mod,0.0)
    elif strat==3:
       A=rfft(a, next_pow2_at_least(n))
       noise = rng.normal(0.0,float(mag)*0.5,A.size) + 1j*rng.normal(0.0,float(mag)*0.5,A.size)
       x=np.real(irfft(A*(1.0+noise), n))
       x=np.maximum(x,0.0)
    elif strat==4:
       # boundary/splines
       t=np.arange(n)/max(1,n-1)
       center=float(rng.uniform(0.2,0.8)); width=float(rng.uniform(0.1,0.3))
       mix=1.0 - float(mag)*np.exp(-((t-center)/width)**2)
       x=np.maximum(a*mix,0.0)
    else:
       # two-sided sine
       t=np.arange(n)/max(1,n)
       mod=1.0 + 0.5*float(mag)*np.cos(2*np.pi)*??? (constant no). Use product of two cos.
       mod=1.0 + float(mag)*np.cos(2*np.pi*rng.uniform(1,4)*t)
       x=np.maximum(a*mod,0.0)
    return normalize_sum(x,S)
```

```
nb = int(rng.integers(4, max(5, min(32, n//2+1))))
```

`make_weights(c,p,gap_frac,rng,wnoise)`:
```
def make_weights(c,p,gap_frac,rng,wnoise=0.05):
    L=int(len(c))
    if L<=0: return np.array([])
    cmax=float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(cmax) or cmax <=0:
       return np.ones(L)/L
    min_active=max(8, min(64, L//32))
    thr=cmax*(1.0 - float(gap_frac))
    mask=c > thr
    cnt=int(np.count_nonzero(mask))
    if cnt < min_active:
       k=min(L-1, L-min_active)
       thr=float(np.partition(c, k)[k])
       mask = c >= thr
    w=np.zeros(L,dtype=np.float64)
    if float(p) <= 0:
       w[mask]=1.0
    else:
       vals=c[mask]
       w[mask]=np.power(vals/cmax, float(p))
    w += 1e-15 / L
    if wnoise is not None and wnoise>0 and rng is not None:
       noise=rng.normal(1.0, float(wnoise), L)
       w=np.maximum(w*noise,0.0)
    s=w.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<=0:
       return np.ones(L)/L
    return w/s
```
`np.partition(c,k)` if k=0 ok. `k` is index of min_active-th from top: e.g., L=10,min_active=3 => k=7, sorted[7] is 3rd largest? yes.

`generate_candidates`:
```
def generate_candidates(a,S,h,eta,q,alpha):
   n=len(a); outs=[]
   h=np.asarray(h,float).ravel()
   hsafe=np.maximum(h,1e-300)
   hsm=(np.roll(hsafe,1)+hsafe+np.roll(hsafe,-1))/3.0
   hsm=np.maximum(hsm,1e-300)
   med=float(np.median(hsm))
   if not np.isfinite(med) or med<=0: med=float(np.mean(hsm))
   ratio=np.clip(med/hsm, 1e-3, 1e3)
   base=ratio**float(q)
   base=np.maximum(base,0.0)
   c1=normalize_sum(a*base,S)
   outs.append(c1)
   dev=hsafe - hsafe.mean()
   ds=float(np.max(np.abs(dev)))
   if ds>1e-15:
       c2=normalize_sum(a*np.exp(np.clip(-float(eta)*dev/ds,-700,700)),S)
       outs.append(c2)
   if alpha > 0:
       inv=normalize_sum(base,S)
       c3=normalize_sum((1-float(alpha))*a + float(alpha)*inv,S)
       outs.append(c3)
   # remove nonfinite
   valid=[]
   seen? no
   for cand in outs:
      if cand.size==n and np.isfinite(cand).all() and cand.sum()>0:
         valid.append(cand)
   return valid
```
Could duplicates; okay.

`optimize_sequence` uses time and prints? Optional.

In `optimize_sequence`, we need avoid too many FFTs if budget very small. Return if budget<3.

Let's code `optimize_sequence` with no nested functions.
```
def optimize_sequence(a_init, budget, rng, normal_print=True):
    a=np.asarray(a_init,float).ravel(); n=len(a)
    if n<8 or budget<1.0:
       S=np.sqrt(2.0*n); a=normalize_sum(a,S); c=fft_convolve_self(a); return a,float(c.max())
    S=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
    a=normalize_sum(a,S)
    c=fft_convolve_self(a)
    cur=float(c.max()); best_obj=cur; best_a=a.copy()
    # initial params based on cur
    if cur > 1.8:
       p_init=1.5; gap_init=0.25; eta=0.2; q=0.6; alpha=0.12
    elif cur > 1.55:
       p_init=6.0; gap_init=0.10; eta=0.12; q=0.35; alpha=0.08
    else:
       p_init=20.0; gap_init=0.025; eta=0.06; q=0.2; alpha=0.05
    gap=end same? We'll blend start/end.
    gap_start=gap_init; gap_end=max(0.003, 0.15*gap_init)
    p_start=p_init; p_end=min(120.0, p_start*5.0+5.0)
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+budget
    it=0; fail=0; stagnation=0; patience=150
    print_interval=2000
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 0.3:
       it+=1
       cur=float(c.max())
       if cur < best_obj - 1e-12:
          best_obj=cur; best_a=a.copy(); stagnation=0
       else:
          stagnation +=1
       if stagnation > patience:
          a=perturb_sequence(best_a,S,rng,float(0.02 + 0.05*(stagnation/patience)))
          c=fft_convolve_self(a); cur=float(c.max())
          # randomize params
          p_cur = float(np.clip((p_start + (p_end-p_start)*0.5)*rng.uniform(0.7,1.3),1.0,300.0))
          gap_cur = float(np.clip(gap_start*0.5*rng.uniform(0.8,1.5),0.003,0.35))
          eta=float(np.clip(eta*rng.uniform(0.8,1.2),0.005,1.0))
          q=float(np.clip(q*rng.uniform(0.7,1.4),0.05,2.0))
          alpha=float(np.clip(alpha*rng.uniform(0.8,1.4),0.01,0.7))
          stagnation=0; fail=0; patience=max(80,min(1500,patience*1.15))
          continue
       elapsed=max(1e-9,time.monotonic()-start)
       progress=min(1.0, elapsed/budget)
       gap_cur = gap_start*(1.0-0.75*progress) + gap_end*(0.75*progress)
       # but if gap_start smaller than end? use max
       if gap_cur < 0.003: gap_cur=0.003
       p_cur = p_start + (p_end-p_start)*(progress*progress)
       wnoise = 0.08*(1.0-progress)
       w=make_weights(c,p_cur,gap_cur,rng,wnoise)
       h=cross_weights_a(w,a)
       cands=generate_candidates(a,S,h,eta,q,alpha)
       improved=False
       # optionally choose candidate order: if fail even, start with cand2/mirror else cand1
       if fail % 2 == 0:
           order=[0,1,2] else [1,0,2] but in Python can't if expression? Use if.
       for idx in order:
           if idx >= len(cands): break
           cand=cands[idx]
           cnew=fft_convolve_self(cand)
           objnew=float(cnew.max())
           if objnew < best_obj:
               best_obj=objnew; best_a=cand.copy()
           # acceptance
           if objnew < cur - 1e-12:
               a=cand; c=cnew; cur=objnew; improved=True; fail=0; break
           # annealing accept
           elif objnew < cur + 0.0005 and stagnation > 50 and rng.random() < 0.05:
               a=cand; c=cnew; cur=objnew; improved=True; fail=0; break
       if improved:
           eta=float(np.clip(eta*1.03,0.003,1.0))
           alpha=float(np.clip(alpha*1.03,0.005,0.7))
           # q maybe increase slightly to be more aggressive if objective high
           q=float(np.clip(q*1.05,0.05,2.0))
           stagnation=0
       else:
           fail +=1
           eta=float(np.clip(eta*0.85,0.001,1.0))
           alpha=float(np.clip(alpha*0.85,0.005,0.7))
           if fail > 20:
              q=float(np.clip(q*rng.uniform(0.6,1.5),0.05,2.0))
              p_cur_adjust = float(np.clip(p_cur*rng.uniform(0.7,1.4),1.0,300.0))
              # cannot persistently change p_cur because p_cur recomputed; we can modify base? We can set a local bias variables? Simpler: no.
              fail=0
           if fail > 80:
              a=perturb_sequence(best_a,S,rng,0.01)
              c=fft_convolve_self(a); cur=float(c.max())
              fail=0
       if it % print_interval ==0 and normal_print:
           print("  opt n=%d it=%d cur=%.6f best=%.6f" % (n,it,cur,best_obj), flush=True)
    return best_a,best_obj
```
The issue: p_cur recomputed from progress and p_start; randomizing p_cur inside fail doesn't persist. We can maintain `p_bias` and `gap_bias`? Simpler: p_cur = (p_start + (p_end-p_start)*progress) * p_mult, where p_mult persists. Initialize p_mult=1, gap_mult=1. Adjust on fail/perturb. Good.

```

def local_perturb_search(a, budget, rng):
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+budget
    n=len(a); S=np.sqrt(2*n); a=normalize_sum(a,S)
    c=fft_convolve_self(a); best_obj=float(c.max()); best_a=a.copy()
    mag=0.02
    fails=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline-0.3:
       for _ in range(min(3, ...)):
          cand=perturb_sequence(a,S,rng,mag)
          c=fft_convolve_self(cand); obj=float(c.max())
          if obj < best_obj - 1e-12:
             best_obj=obj; best_a=cand.copy(); a=cand; c=cconv?; fails=0; mag=min(0.05,mag*1.1)
             break
       else:
          fails+=1; mag=max(0.001,mag*0.98)
          if fails>200: mag=0.03; fails=0
    return best_a,best_obj
```

`generate_inits(n,rng)` top-level. Ensure returns list of arrays length n *not normalized*. Use vector t.
```
def generate_inits(n,rng):
   out=[]
   x=np.arange(n,dtype=float)
   t=x/(n-1) if n>1 else np.array([0])
   # geometric
   for alpha in [0.3,0.6,1.0,1.5,2.5,4.0,6.0]:
       r=1.0 - alpha/(n-1.0) if n>1 else 1.0
       if r <=0:
           arr=np.zeros(n); arr[0]=1.0
       else:
           arr=np.power(r,x)
       out.append(arr)
       out.append(arr[::-1].copy())
   # power
   for beta in [0.25,0.6,1.0]:
       out.append(np.power(x+1.0,-beta))
       out.append(np.power(n+1.0-x,-beta))
   # gaussians
   for center in (0.2,0.4,0.6,0.8):
       for sigma in (0.15,0.3):
           out.append(np.exp(-0.5*((x-n*center)/(sigma*n))**2))
   # random periodic
   for _ in range(4):
       f1=float(rng.uniform(0.2,3.0)); f2=float(rng.uniform(3.0,12.0))
       a1=float(rng.uniform(0.1,0.7)); a2=float(rng.uniform(0.05,0.35))
       p1=float(rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)); p2=float(rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
       arr=1.0 + a1*np.cos(2*np.pi*f1*t + p1) + a2*np.sin(2*np.pi*f2*t + p2)
       out.append(np.maximum(arr,0.01))
   return out
```
Many inits (~50). Evaluating each conv may be heavy if n=16384 (50*2 conv? just one) but okay maybe 1-2s? 50*0.02=1s. But memory list of arrays large; okay. We can limit to 20 if n large.
```
max_inits = 12 if main_len>12000 else 24
```
Truncate.

```python
"""
Search for non-negative coefficient sequences minimizing

    2 * n * max(a * a) / (sum(a))**2

with a mirror/inverse-overlap solver, active-set weights, embedded restarts,
and multi-start polish.  The code is deliberately self-contained and robust:
it uses FFT for internal objectives, keeps the best sequence for the actual
evaluate_sequence contract, and falls back safely to a uniform sequence.
"""
import time
import numpy as np
from scipy.fft import rfft, irfft


def _next_pow2_at_least(m):
    m = int(m)
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def _fft_convolve_self(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    size = 2 * n - 1
    fsize = _next_pow2_at_least(size)
    A = rfft(a, fsize)
    c = irfft(A * A, fsize)
    c = c[:size]
    # Numerical roundoff can create tiny negatives for non-negative inputs.
    return np.maximum(c, 0.0)


def _cross_weights_a(w, a):
    """
    For weights w over full convolution indices and vector a, compute
        h_i = sum_k w_k * a_{k-i}
    for i=0..n-1.  This is the gradient factor (without 2) for the weighted
    convolution sum.
    """
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    fsize = _next_pow2_at_least(2 * n - 1)
    W = rfft(w, fsize)
    A = rfft(a, fsize)
    h = irfft(W * np.conj(A), fsize)[:n]
    return np.maximum(h, 0.0)


def _normalize_sum(a, S):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-15:
        if a.size == 0:
            return a
        return np.full(a.size, S / float(a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return a * (float(S) / s)


def _resample_seq(a, m):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    m = int(m)
    if m <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0 or n == m:
        return a.copy()
    if n == 1:
        return np.full(m, float(a.sum()) / float(m), dtype=np.float64)
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, float(n - 1), n)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, float(m - 1), m)
    return np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)


def _internal_objective(a):
    """
    Same as the evaluator's objective up to summation scale:
    normalize sum to sqrt(2n), then the objective equals max(a*a).
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    if n <= 0:
        return float("inf")
    S = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * n))
    a = _normalize_sum(a, S)
    c = _fft_convolve_self(a)
    return float(c.max())


def _make_weights(c, p, gap_frac, rng, wnoise=0.05):
    """
    Active-set/near-active weights.  p focuses on the most overloaded
    convolution coefficients.  gap_frac controls the width of the active set.
    """
    L = int(c.size)
    if L <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    cmax = float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(cmax) or cmax <= 0.0:
        return np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64) / L

    min_active = max(8, min(64, L // 32))
    gap = float(gap_frac)
    if gap <= 0.0:
        gap = 0.01
    if gap > 0.95:
        gap = 0.95

    thr = cmax * (1.0 - gap)
    mask = c > thr
    cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(mask))
    if cnt < min_active:
        k = L - min_active
        if k < 0:
            k = 0
        if k >= L:
            k = L - 1
        thr = float(np.partition(c, k)[k])
        mask = c >= thr

    w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    if p <= 0.0:
        w[mask] = 1.0
    else:
        vals = c[mask]
        # Use relative power to keep weights stable across normalizations.
        w[mask] = np.power(vals / cmax, float(p))

    # Tiny all-coordinate floor prevents perfectly zero overlap regions from
    # becoming singular in inverse-overlap updates.
    w += 1e-15 / float(L)

    if wnoise is not None and wnoise > 0.0 and rng is not None:
        noise = np.maximum(1.0 + rng.normal(0.0, float(wnoise), L), 1e-12)
        w = np.maximum(w * noise, 0.0)

    s = float(w.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64) / L
    return w / s


def _generate_candidates(a, S, h, eta, q, alpha):
    """
    Produce several non-negative update candidates:
      - inverse-overlap ratio (strong redistribution),
      - centered exponential mirror update,
      - linear blend with a pure inverse-overlap direction.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return []

    hsafe = np.maximum(h, 1e-300)

    # Light circular smoothing to avoid pathological one-coordinate spikes.
    hsm = (np.roll(hsafe, 1) + hsafe + np.roll(hsafe, -1)) / 3.0
    hsm = np.maximum(hsm, 1e-300)

    med = float(np.median(hsm))
    if not np.isfinite(med) or med <= 0.0:
        med = float(hsafe.mean())

    ratio = np.clip(med / hsm, 1e-3, 1e3)
    base = np.power(ratio, float(q))
    base = np.maximum(base, 0.0)

    outs = []

    cand1 = _normalize_sum(a * base, S)
    outs.append(cand1)

    dev = hsafe - hsafe.mean()
    ds = float(np.max(np.abs(dev))) if dev.size else 1.0
    if ds > 1e-15:
        z = np.clip(-float(eta) * dev / ds, -700.0, 700.0)
        cand2 = _normalize_sum(a * np.exp(z), S)
        outs.append(cand2)

    if alpha > 0.0:
        inv = _normalize_sum(base, S)
        cand3 = _normalize_sum((1.0 - float(alpha)) * a + float(alpha) * inv, S)
        outs.append(cand3)

    valid = []
    for cand in outs:
        if cand.size == n and np.all(np.isfinite(cand)) and float(cand.sum()) > 0.0:
            valid.append(cand)
    return valid


def _perturb_sequence(a, S, rng, mag):
    """Structured low-noise perturbations to escape local minima."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return a
    mag = float(mag)
    if not np.isfinite(mag) or mag <= 0.0:
        mag = 0.01

    strat = int(rng.integers(0, 7))

    if strat == 0:
        loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300))
        loga = loga + rng.normal(0.0, mag, n)
        loga = loga - loga.max()
        x = np.exp(np.clip(loga, -700.0, 700.0))

    elif strat == 1:
        t = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / max(1, n)
        f = float(rng.uniform(0.3, 6.0))
        ph = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        mod = 1.0 + mag * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * f * t + ph)
        x = np.maximum(a * mod, 0.0)

    elif strat == 2:
        lo = 4
        hi = max(5, min(32, n // 2 + 1))
        if hi <= lo:
            hi = lo + 1
        nb = int(rng.integers(lo, hi))
        mods = rng.uniform(1.0 - mag, 1.0 + mag, nb)
        mod = np.repeat(mods, n // nb + 1)[:n]
        x = np.maximum(a * mod, 0.0)

    elif strat == 3:
        fsize = _next_pow2_at_least(n)
        A = rfft(a, fsize)
        noise = rng.normal(0.0, 0.5 * mag, A.size) + 1j * rng.normal(0.0, 0.5 * mag, A.size)
        x = np.real(irfft(A * (1.0 + noise), n))
        x = np.maximum(x, 0.0)

    elif strat == 4:
        t = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / max(1, n - 1)
        center = float(rng.uniform(0.20, 0.80))
        width = float(rng.uniform(0.10, 0.35))
        mod = 1.0 - mag * np.exp(-((t - center) / width) ** 2)
        x = np.maximum(a * mod, 0.0)

    elif strat == 5:
        # Slight antisymmetry-breaking update: lower right tail.
        t = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / max(1, n - 1)
        mod = np.maximum(1.0 - mag * t, 0.01)
        x = np.maximum(a * mod, 0.0)

    else:
        # Mix with a simple geometric template.
        r = float(rng.uniform(0.75, 0.999))
        tpl = np.power(r, np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64))
        tpl = _normalize_sum(tpl, S)
        beta = float(rng.uniform(0.10, 0.45))
        x = (1.0 - beta) * a + beta * tpl
        x = np.maximum(x, 0.0)

    return _normalize_sum(x, S)


def _generate_inits(n, rng):
    """A collection of deterministic and random positive templates."""
    out = []
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return out

    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n > 1:
        t = x / float(n - 1)
    else:
        t = np.array([0.0], dtype=np.float64)

    # Geometric decay (left/right).
    for alpha in (0.3, 0.6, 1.0, 1.5, 2.5, 4.0, 6.0):
        if n > 1:
            r = 1.0 - alpha / float(n - 1)
        else:
            r = 1.0
        if r <= 0.0:
            arr = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            arr[0] = 1.0
        else:
            arr = np.power(r, x)
        out.append(arr)
        out.append(arr[::-1].copy())

    # Power-law decays.
    for beta in (0.25, 0.6, 1.0):
        out.append(np.power(x + 1.0, -beta))
        out.append(np.power(float(n + 1) - x, -beta))

    # Smooth bumps.
    for center in (0.20, 0.40, 0.60, 0.80):
        for sigma in (0.15, 0.30):
            out.append(np.exp(-0.5 * ((x - n * center) / (sigma * n)) ** 2))

    # Random low-frequency modulations.
    for _ in range(5):
        f1 = float(rng.uniform(0.2, 3.0))
        f2 = float(rng.uniform(3.0, 12.0))
        a1 = float(rng.uniform(0.10, 0.70))
        a2 = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.35))
        p1 = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        p2 = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        arr = 1.0 + a1 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * f1 * t + p1) + a2 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * f2 * t + p2)
        out.append(np.maximum(arr, 0.01))

    return out


def _optimize_sequence(a_init, budget, rng, verbose=False):
    """
    Main local solver.  Objective is the raw max convolution after normalization
    to sum sqrt(2n).  The sequence length stays fixed.
    """
    a_init = np.asarray(a_init, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a_init.size)
    if n < 8 or budget <= 0.0:
        S = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * max(1, n)))
        a = _normalize_sum(a_init, S)
        c = _fft_convolve_self(a)
        return a, float(c.max())

    S = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * n))
    a = _normalize_sum(a_init, S)
    c = _fft_convolve_self(a)
    cur = float(c.max())
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_obj = cur

    # Initial hyperparameters depending on how rough the starting point is.
    if cur > 1.80:
        p_start = 1.5
        p_end = 80.0
        gap_start = 0.25
        gap_end = 0.004
        eta = 0.22
        q = 0.60
        alpha = 0.12
    elif cur > 1.55:
        p_start = 6.0
        p_end = 120.0
        gap_start = 0.10
        gap_end = 0.004
        eta = 0.12
        q = 0.35
        alpha = 0.08
    else:
        p_start = 20.0
        p_end = 220.0
        gap_start = 0.025
        gap_end = 0.003
        eta = 0.06
        q = 0.22
        alpha = 0.05

    p_mult = 1.0
    gap_mult = 1.0

    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(0.5, float(budget))
    it = 0
    fail = 0
    stagnation = 0
    patience = 140

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 0.25:
        it += 1
        cur = float(c.max())

        if cur < best_obj - 1e-12:
            best_obj = cur
            best_a = a.copy()
            stagnation = 0
        else:
            stagnation += 1

        # Hard restart from the best known point if stuck.
        if stagnation > patience:
            mag = 0.02 + 0.06 * min(1.0, float(stagnation) / float(patience))
            a = _perturb_sequence(best_a, S, rng, mag)
            c = _fft_convolve_self(a)
            cur = float(c.max())
            p_mult = float(np.clip(rng.uniform(0.5, 2.0), 0.2, 8.0))
            gap_mult = float(np.clip(rng.uniform(0.5, 2.0), 0.2, 5.0))
            eta = float(np.clip(eta * rng.uniform(0.8, 1.2), 0.005, 1.0))
            q = float(np.clip(q * rng.uniform(0.75, 1.35), 0.05, 2.0))
            alpha = float(np.clip(alpha * rng.uniform(0.8, 1.35), 0.008, 0.65))
            fail = 0
            stagnation = 0
            patience = max(80, min(1600, int(patience * 1.15)))
            continue

        elapsed = max(1e-9, time.monotonic() - start)
        progress = min(1.0, elapsed / max(1e-9, float(budget)))

        base_p = p_start + (p_end - p_start) * (progress * progress)
        base_gap = gap_start + (gap_end - gap_start) * progress
        p_cur = max(0.1, base_p * max(0.1, p_mult))
        gap_cur = max(0.003, base_gap * max(0.1, gap_mult))
        wnoise = 0.08 * (1.0 - progress)

        w = _make_weights(c, p_cur, gap_cur, rng, wnoise)
        h = _cross_weights_a(w, a)
        cands = _generate_candidates(a, S, h, eta, q, alpha)
        if len(cands) == 0:
            # Very defensive fallback.
            a = _perturb_sequence(best_a, S, rng, 0.02)
            c = _fft_convolve_self(a)
            cur = float(c.max())
            continue

        if fail % 2 == 0:
            order = (0, 1, 2)
        else:
            order = (1, 0, 2)

        improved = False
        for idx in order:
            if idx >= len(cands):
                break
            cand = cands[idx]
            cnew = _fft_convolve_self(cand)
            objnew = float(cnew.max())
            if not np.isfinite(objnew):
                continue

            if objnew < best_obj:
                best_obj = objnew
                best_a = cand.copy()

            # Normal descent acceptance, plus rare small annealing moves.
            if objnew < cur - 1e-12:
                a = cand
                c = cnew
                cur = objnew
                improved = True
                fail = 0
                break

            if (objnew < cur + 5e-4 and stagnation > 60 and float(rng.random()) < 0.04):
                a = cand
                c = cnew
                cur = objnew
                improved = True
                fail = 0
                break

        if improved:
            eta = float(np.clip(eta * 1.03, 0.003, 1.0))
            alpha = float(np.clip(alpha * 1.03, 0.005, 0.65))
            q = float(np.clip(q * 1.04, 0.05, 2.0))
            stagnation = 0
        else:
            fail += 1
            eta = float(np.clip(eta * 0.85, 0.001, 1.0))
            alpha = float(np.clip(alpha * 0.85, 0.005, 0.65))
            if fail > 18:
                p_mult = float(np.clip(p_mult * rng.uniform(0.65, 1.45), 0.2, 8.0))
                gap_mult = float(np.clip(gap_mult * rng.uniform(0.65, 1.45), 0.2, 5.0))
                q = float(np.clip(q * rng.uniform(0.65, 1.45), 0.05, 2.0))
                fail = 0
            if fail > 80:
                a = _perturb_sequence(best_a, S, rng, 0.012)
                c = _fft_convolve_self(a)
                cur = float(c.max())
                fail = 0

        if verbose and it % 2500 == 0:
            print("    opt n=%d it=%d cur=%.7f best=%.7f" % (n, it, cur, best_obj), flush=True)

    return best_a, best_obj


def _local_perturb_search(a_init, budget, rng):
    """
    Final low-amplitude stochastic polish around the best sequence.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a_init, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    if n <= 0 or budget <= 0.0:
        return a, float("inf")

    S = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * n))
    a = _normalize_sum(a, S)
    c = _fft_convolve_self(a)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_obj = float(c.max())

    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(0.5, float(budget))

    mag = 0.018
    fails = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 0.25:
        cand = _perturb_sequence(a, S, rng, mag)
        c = _fft_convolve_self(cand)
        obj = float(c.max())

        if np.isfinite(obj) and obj < best_obj - 1e-12:
            best_obj = obj
            best_a = cand.copy()
            a = cand
            c = _fft_convolve_self(a)
            fails = 0
            mag = min(0.05, mag * 1.09)
        else:
            fails += 1
            mag = max(0.0012, mag * 0.985)
            if fails > 250:
                mag = 0.023
                fails = 0

    return best_a, best_obj


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400.0):
    """Return the best non-negative sequence found within the time budget."""
    seed = int(seed)
    budget_s = float(budget_s)
    if not np.isfinite(budget_s) or budget_s < 30.0:
        budget_s = 30.0

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    reserve = 130.0 if budget_s > 600.0 else 35.0
    deadline = t0 + max(60.0, budget_s - reserve)

    best_a = None
    best_obj = float("inf")

    # Load the previously supplied construction if available.
    prev = None
    try:
        prev = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if prev.size < 32:
            prev = None
        else:
            prev = np.clip(prev, 0.0, 1000.0)
    except Exception:
        prev = None

    # Choose the primary length.  Keep it in a range that is search- and
    # evaluator-friendly.
    if prev is not None and 2048 <= int(prev.size) <= 16384:
        main_len = int(prev.size)
    else:
        main_len = 8192

    if main_len < 256:
        main_len = 8192
    if main_len > 16384:
        main_len = 16384

    print("propose_candidate start: budget=%.1fs main_len=%d seed=%d" % (budget_s, main_len, seed), flush=True)

    S_main = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * main_len))

    inits_list = []

    # Uniform fallback/anchor.
    inits_list.append(np.ones(main_len, dtype=np.float64))

    # Previous solution and mildly perturbed variants.
    if prev is not None:
        prev_main = _resample_seq(prev, main_len)
        inits_list.append(prev_main)
        inits_list.append(prev_main[::-1].copy())
        inits_list.append(_perturb_sequence(prev_main, S_main, rng, 0.06))
        inits_list.append(_perturb_sequence(prev_main, S_main, rng, 0.16))

    # Generated templates.
    templates = _generate_inits(main_len, rng)
    max_templates = 14 if main_len >= 12000 else 22
    templates = templates[:max_templates]
    inits_list.extend(templates)

    # Evaluate initial templates quickly and sort by internal objective.
    evaluated = []
    idx = 0
    for arr in inits_list:
        arr = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if arr.size != main_len:
            arr = _resample_seq(arr, main_len)
        arr = _normalize_sum(arr, S_main)
        c = _fft_convolve_self(arr)
        obj = float(c.max())
        if np.isfinite(obj):
            evaluated.append((obj, idx, arr))
        idx += 1

    if len(evaluated) == 0:
        arr = np.ones(main_len, dtype=np.float64)
        S = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * main_len))
        arr = _normalize_sum(arr, S)
        c = _fft_convolve_self(arr)
        evaluated.append((float(c.max()), 0, arr))

    evaluated.sort()

    # Main search: allocate most time to the best initializations.
    # Reserve last segment for polish and a possible larger-length experiment.
    time_left = dev = None  # placeholder silence? (removed in code below)
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 220.0 and evaluated:
        obj0, idx0, arr0 = evaluated.pop(0)

        time_left = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if time_left <= 220.0:
            break

        remaining_main = time_left - 220.0
        if remaining_main <= 5.0:
            break

        # Allocate a slice.  Pop order already prioritizes better starts.
        if idx0 == 0:
            frac = 0.42
        elif idx0 == 1:
            frac = 0.24
        elif idx0 == 2:
            frac = 0.14
        elif idx0 == 3:
            frac = 0.09
        elif idx0 == 4:
            frac = 0.05
        elif idx0 == 5:
            frac = 0.03
        else:
            frac = 0.02

        t_budget = min(max(8.0, remaining_main * frac), max(8.0, time_left - 230.0))
        if t_budget <= 2.0:
            continue

        print("  optimizing init idx=%d initial_obj=%.7f budget=%.1fs" % (idx0, obj0, t_budget), flush=True)
        a_opt, s_opt = _optimize_sequence(arr0, t_budget, rng, verbose=(idx0 < 3))

        if np.isfinite(s_opt) and s_opt < best_obj:
            best_obj = s_opt
            best_a = a_opt.copy()
            print("    improved best to %.7f (len=%d elapsed=%.1fs)" % (best_obj, best_a.size, time.monotonic() - t0), flush=True)

    # If we have time, try a larger length by resampling the best candidate.
    if best_a is not None and best_a.size > 0:
        time_left = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if time_left > 160:
            alt_len = int(min(16384, max(best_a.size * 2, 16384)))
            if alt_len != best_a.size and alt_len <= 16384:
                t_alt = min(120.0, time_left - 120.0)
                if t_alt > 40.0:
                    alt0 = _resample_seq(best_a, alt_len)
                    S_alt = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * alt_len))
                    alt0 = _normalize_sum(alt0, S_alt)
                    print("  trying larger length %d for %.1fs" % (alt_len, t_alt), flush=True)
                    a_alt, s_alt = _optimize_sequence(alt0, t_alt, rng, verbose=True)
                    if np.isfinite(s_alt) and s_alt < best_obj:
                        best_obj = s_alt
                        best_a = a_alt.copy()
                        print("    larger length improved to %.7f" % best_obj, flush=True)

    # Final polish: a short narrow optimizer run and stochastic local search.
    if best_a is None:
        n_fb = 8192
        S_fb = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * n_fb))
        best_a = np.full(n_fb, S_fb / float(n_fb), dtype=np.float64)
        best_obj = float(_internal_objective(best_a))
        print("fallback uniform;", flush=True)

    time_left = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if time_left > 45.0:
        t_narrow = max(20.0, min(160.0, 0.45 * time_left))
        print("  final narrow optimizer %.1fs" % t_narrow, flush=True)
        a_p, s_p = _optimize_sequence(best_a, t_narrow, rng, verbose=True)
        if np.isfinite(s_p) and s_p < best_obj:
            best_obj = s_p
            best_a = a_p.copy()

    time_left = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if time_left > 35.0:
        t_local = max(15.0, min(300.0, time_left - 15.0))
        print("  final local perturb polish %.1fs" % t_local, flush=True)
        a_l, s_l = _local_perturb_search(best_a, t_local, rng)
        if np.isfinite(s_l) and s_l < best_obj:
            best_obj = s_l
            best_a = a_l.copy()

    # Keep final length evaluator-friendly.
    if best_a is not None and best_a.size > 16384:
        best_a = _resample_seq(best_a, 16384)
        S = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * best_a.size))
        best_a = _normalize_sum(best_a, S)
        best_obj = float(_internal_objective(best_a))

    # Ensure valid positive output.
    final_seq = [float(x) for x in np.clip(best_a, 0.0, 1000.0)]
    if not final_seq:
        n_fb = 8192
        S_fb = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * n_fb))
        final_seq = [float(S_fb / float(n_fb))] * n_fb

    # Verification against the official evaluator, if available.
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence(final_seq)
        if np.isfinite(val):
            print("official verification obj=%.7f internal=%.7f len=%d" % (float(val), float(best_obj), len(final_seq)), flush=True)
            if float(val) < float(best_obj):
                best_obj = float(val)
        else:
            print("official verification returned non-finite; returning internally valid sequence", flush=True)
    except Exception as e:
        print("official verification unavailable/failed: %s" % repr(e), flush=True)

    print("returning len=%d obj=%.7f elapsed=%.1fs" % (len(final_seq), float(best_obj), time.monotonic() - t0), flush=True)
    return final_seq
```
